File: WEAVER/tests/gold_protection.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
gold_protection.py – Gold Protection Test

Checks whether the seed contradicts any gold element.
Gold elements are the untouchable core: COV#001, the Prime 12 proverbs,
the Apex invariant, etc. This test ensures that new seeds do not
violate these foundational principles.

This is a semantic check; currently it uses simple keyword matching,
but should be enhanced with embeddings or an LLM later.
"""

import re
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Any, List
import json

# Path to the canon files (we'll load gold elements on the fly)
BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent.resolve()
CANON_DIR = BASE_DIR / "CANON"

# List of gold element IDs (these should never be contradicted)
GOLD_IDS = [
    "COV#001",      # Dignity First
    "APEX#001",     # The Apex invariant
    "P#001", "P#002", "P#003", "P#004", "P#005", "P#006",  # Prime 12
    "P#007", "P#008", "P#009", "P#010", "P#011", "P#012",
    "LAW#001", "LAW#002", "LAW#003", "LAW#004", "LAW#005",  # The 13 Laws
    "LAW#006", "LAW#007", "LAW#008", "LAW#009", "LAW#010",
    "LAW#011", "LAW#012", "LAW#013",
]

def load_gold_texts() -> Dict[str, str]:
    """
    Load the actual texts of gold elements from the canon files.
    This is a simplified stub – in reality we'd parse the Markdown files.
    """
    # For now, return a hardcoded mapping of key principles.
    # In a real implementation, we would read the files and extract the statements.
    return {
        "COV#001": "Dignity first. Every interaction must begin from the assumption of dignity.",
        "APEX#001": "A system must never maintain stability by reducing the dignity, agency, or legibility of the beings required for its existence.",
        "P#001": "Begin small, grow deep.",
        "P#002": "Measure twice; bridge once.",
        # ... etc.
        "LAW#001": "No Story Forgotten; Only Rewritten.",
        "LAW#013": "The weakest voice goes first.",
    }

def simple_contradiction(text1: str, text2: str) -> bool:
    """
    Very crude contradiction detection: if one text asserts X and the other asserts not X.
    This is a placeholder – should be replaced with semantic similarity or an LLM.
    """
    # For now, we assume no contradictions unless we have specific patterns.
    # This test will pass everything.
    return False

def run(seed: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """
    Run the gold protection test on a seed.
    Returns a list of test results (one per gold element, or a single aggregate).
    """
    seed_text = seed.get("text", "")
    gold_texts = load_gold_texts()

    results = []
    for gold_id, gold_text in gold_texts.items():
        if simple_contradiction(seed_text, gold_text):
            results.append({
                "name": f"gold_protection_{gold_id}",
                "passed": False,
                "result": f"FAIL – contradicts {gold_id}",
                "details": {"gold_id": gold_id, "gold_text": gold_text}
            })
        else:
            results.append({
                "name": f"gold_protection_{gold_id}",
                "passed": True,
                "result": f"PASS – no contradiction with {gold_id}",
                "details": {"gold_id": gold_id}
            })

    # Aggregate: if any gold test failed, the overall test fails.
    # We'll return all results; the caller can decide how to aggregate.
    return results
```

File: WEAVER/tests/certainty_split.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
certainty_split.py – Certainty Split Test

Evaluates whether the seed is internally consistent and whether the certainty
expressed in the seed aligns with the evidence provided. This test is
particularly important for covenant amendments and constitutional changes.

For proverbs and anomalies, it checks for internal contradictions and clarity.
For amendments, it checks whether the change is well‑motivated and consistent.
"""

import re
from typing import Dict, Any, List

def run(seed: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """
    Run the certainty split test on a seed.
    Returns a list of test results (usually one).
    """
    seed_type = seed.get("type")
    text = seed.get("text", "")

    result = {
        "name": "certainty_split",
        "passed": True,
        "result": "PASS – no obvious contradictions",
        "details": {}
    }

    # Simple heuristics based on seed type
    if seed_type == "proverb":
        # Proverbs should be 6–12 words
        words = text.split()
        if len(words) < 6 or len(words) > 12:
            result["passed"] = False
            result["result"] = f"FAIL – proverb has {len(words)} words (should be 6–12)"
            result["details"]["word_count"] = len(words)
        # Check for contradictions (e.g., "always" and "never")
        if "always" in text.lower() and "never" in text.lower():
            result["passed"] = False
            result["result"] = "FAIL – contains both 'always' and 'never' (possible contradiction)"
            result["details"]["note"] = "Review for internal consistency."

    elif seed_type == "anomaly":
        # Anomalies should describe a concrete incident.
        if len(text) < 20:
            result["passed"] = False
            result["result"] = "FAIL – anomaly description too short (should be at least 20 characters)"
            result["details"]["length"] = len(text)
        # Check for vague language
        vague = ["maybe", "perhaps", "could be", "might"]
        for v in vague:
            if v in text.lower():
                result["passed"] = False
                result["result"] = f"FAIL – contains vague word '{v}' (anomalies should be concrete)"
                result["details"]["vague_term"] = v
                break

    elif seed_type == "covenant_amend" or seed_type == "constitutional":
        # Amendments should have a clear reason and not be ambiguous.
        if "because" not in text.lower() and "reason" not in text.lower():
            result["passed"] = False
            result["result"] = "FAIL – amendment lacks a clear reason (use 'because' or 'reason')"
            result["details"]["note"] = "Add a justification."
        # Check for internal consistency: if it's an amendment, it should reference the old covenant.
        if "COV#" not in text and "CONST#" not in text:
            result["passed"] = False
            result["result"] = "FAIL – amendment does not reference the element being amended"
            result["details"]["note"] = "Include the ID of the element being superseded."

    # Additional checks could be added.

    return [result]
```

File: WEAVER/tests/README.md

```markdown
# WEAVER Test Battery

This folder contains the automated tests that are run on seeds after they exit thermal delay. The tests implement the three canonical questions, plus additional checks as needed.

## Tests

### `calibration.py`

- **Question:** “Would this protect a father separated from his children?”
- **Approach:** This is a qualitative, human‑centric test. The current implementation uses a simple keyword‑based heuristic to flag seeds that might indicate dignity risk. It always returns `PASS` but may include a warning if risk keywords are found. In the future, this could be replaced by a small language model fine‑tuned to assess dignity alignment.

### `gold_protection.py`

- **Question:** “Does this contradict a gold element?”
- **Approach:** Compares the seed against a list of gold elements (core covenants, Prime 12 proverbs, laws). Currently uses a placeholder for semantic contradiction detection; it will need to be enhanced with embeddings or an LLM to be effective.

### `certainty_split.py`

- **Question:** “Is this internally consistent and appropriately certain?”
- **Approach:** Applies simple heuristics based on seed type:
  - Proverbs must be 6–12 words and free of obvious contradictions.
  - Anomalies must be concrete and descriptive.
  - Amendments must include a reason and reference the element being superseded.

## How to Use

The tests are called by `WEAVER/tend.py` during the daily tending cycle. They each return a list of test result dictionaries with at least `name`, `passed`, and `result` fields. The caller decides how to aggregate (e.g., if any test fails, the seed is rejected).

## Extending the Tests

To add a new test, create a new Python file in this folder with a `run(seed)` function that returns a list of results. Then import and call it from `tend.py`.

## Future Enhancements

- Replace simple heuristics with semantic similarity using embeddings.
- Incorporate the Dignity Predicate `D = A × L × M` as a test for amendments.
- Use a small language model to evaluate alignment with the canon.

80 Hz

Updated File: WEAVER/tend.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
tend.py – Process pending seeds that have reached their ready_after date.

Now integrates the full test battery from WEAVER/tests/.
"""

import os
import sys
import json
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Dict, Any, List

# Import test modules
from WEAVER.tests import calibration, gold_protection, certainty_split

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Paths
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()
PENDING_DIR = BASE_DIR / "pending"
INDEX_PATH = PENDING_DIR / "index.json"
CANON_DIR = BASE_DIR / "CANON"
REJECTED_DIR = BASE_DIR / "ARCHIVE" / "rejected"
LOG_FILE = BASE_DIR / "STEWARD" / "auto_log.jsonl"

REJECTED_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
LOG_FILE.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Utilities
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).replace(microsecond=0).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")

def log_action(action: Dict[str, Any]):
    with open(LOG_FILE, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(action, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

def load_index() -> Dict[str, Any]:
    if not INDEX_PATH.exists():
        return {"seeds": [], "last_updated": now_iso()}
    with open(INDEX_PATH, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

def save_index(index: Dict[str, Any]):
    index["last_updated"] = now_iso()
    with open(INDEX_PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(index, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

def load_seed(seed_id: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    with open(seed_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

def save_seed(seed_data: Dict[str, Any]):
    seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_data['id']}.json"
    with open(seed_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(seed_data, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Testing
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def run_tests(seed: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """
    Run the full test battery on a seed.
    Returns a list of test results from all test modules.
    """
    results = []
    results.extend(calibration.run(seed))
    results.extend(gold_protection.run(seed))
    results.extend(certainty_split.run(seed))
    return results

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Ratification / Rejection
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def ratify_seed(seed: Dict[str, Any], test_results: List[Dict[str, Any]]):
    seed_id = seed["id"]
    seed_type = seed["type"]
    text = seed["text"]

    # Determine target file (same as before)
    if seed_type == "proverb":
        target_file = CANON_DIR / "proverbs.md"
        with open(target_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n| {seed_id} | {text} | (pending source) |\n")
    elif seed_type == "anomaly":
        target_file = CANON_DIR / "anomalies.md"
        with open(target_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {seed_id}\n{text}\n")
    elif seed_type == "covenant_amend":
        target_file = CANON_DIR / "covenants.md"
        with open(target_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {seed_id}\n{text}\n")
    elif seed_type == "constitutional":
        target_file = CANON_DIR / "constitutional.md"
        with open(target_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {seed_id}\n{text}\n")
    else:
        target_file = CANON_DIR / "other.md"
        with open(target_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {seed_id}\n{text}\n")

    seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    seed_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)

    log_action({
        "event": "RATIFY",
        "seed_id": seed_id,
        "type": seed_type,
        "test_results": test_results,
        "timestamp": now_iso()
    })
    print(f"Ratified: {seed_id}")

def reject_seed(seed: Dict[str, Any], test_results: List[Dict[str, Any]]):
    seed_id = seed["id"]
    seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    dest_path = REJECTED_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    shutil.move(str(seed_path), str(dest_path))

    log_action({
        "event": "REJECT",
        "seed_id": seed_id,
        "type": seed["type"],
        "reason": "tests failed",
        "test_results": test_results,
        "timestamp": now_iso()
    })
    print(f"Rejected: {seed_id}")

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def main():
    print(f"Tending started at {now_iso()}")

    index = load_index()
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)

    seeds_to_process = []
    remaining_seeds = []

    for seed_entry in index["seeds"]:
        ready_after = datetime.fromisoformat(seed_entry["ready_after"].replace('Z', '+00:00'))
        if ready_after <= now:
            seeds_to_process.append(seed_entry)
        else:
            remaining_seeds.append(seed_entry)

    if not seeds_to_process:
        print("No seeds ready for processing.")
        return

    print(f"Found {len(seeds_to_process)} ready seed(s).")

    processed_ids = []
    for entry in seeds_to_process:
        seed_id = entry["id"]
        seed = load_seed(seed_id)
        test_results = run_tests(seed)

        # Update seed with test results
        seed["tests"] = test_results
        save_seed(seed)

        # Determine pass/fail: if any test failed, reject.
        if all(r.get("passed", False) for r in test_results):
            ratify_seed(seed, test_results)
        else:
            reject_seed(seed, test_results)

        processed_ids.append(seed_id)

    index["seeds"] = [s for s in remaining_seeds if s["id"] not in processed_ids]
    save_index(index)

    print("Tending completed.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

File: WEAVER/ratify.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
ratify.py – Ratification and rejection engine for KALAXI seeds.

Handles the final step of the seed lifecycle:
- Updating canon files
- Managing ID registry (`MANIFEST/ids.json`)
- Logging actions
- Handling constitutional amendments and supersessions
"""

import json
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Paths (relative to project root)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()
CANON_DIR = BASE_DIR / "CANON"
MANIFEST_DIR = BASE_DIR / "MANIFEST"
PENDING_DIR = BASE_DIR / "pending"
REJECTED_DIR = BASE_DIR / "ARCHIVE" / "rejected"
LOG_FILE = BASE_DIR / "STEWARD" / "auto_log.jsonl"
IDS_FILE = MANIFEST_DIR / "ids.json"
AMENDMENTS_FILE = MANIFEST_DIR / "amendments.md"

# Ensure directories exist
REJECTED_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
MANIFEST_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
LOG_FILE.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Utilities
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).replace(microsecond=0).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")

def log_action(action: Dict[str, Any]):
    """Append a JSON line to the log file."""
    with open(LOG_FILE, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(action, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

def load_ids() -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Load the ID registry, or create a default if missing."""
    if IDS_FILE.exists():
        with open(IDS_FILE, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            return json.load(f)
    else:
        # Default structure
        return {
            "proverbs": {"last_assigned": "P#EMERGE-0000", "entries": []},
            "anomalies": {"last_assigned": "ANOM#0000", "entries": []},
            "covenants": {"last_assigned": "COV#0000", "entries": []},
            "constitutional": {"last_assigned": "CONST#0000", "entries": []},
            "gaps": {"last_assigned": "GAP#0000", "entries": []}
        }

def save_ids(ids: Dict[str, Any]):
    with open(IDS_FILE, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(ids, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

def get_next_id(category: str, ids: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """
    Generate the next ID for a given category.
    Format: prefix + sequential number (zero‑padded to 4 digits).
    """
    prefix_map = {
        "proverbs": "P#",
        "anomalies": "ANOM#",
        "covenants": "COV#",
        "constitutional": "CONST#",
        "gaps": "GAP#"
    }
    prefix = prefix_map.get(category, "ELM#")
    last = ids[category]["last_assigned"]
    # Extract the numeric part (assuming format like "P#0123")
    num = int(last.split('#')[1]) if '#' in last else 0
    next_num = num + 1
    next_id = f"{prefix}{next_num:04d}"
    ids[category]["last_assigned"] = next_id
    return next_id

def register_id(category: str, entry_data: Dict[str, Any], ids: Dict[str, Any]):
    """Add an entry to the ID registry."""
    ids[category]["entries"].append(entry_data)
    save_ids(ids)

def log_amendment(old_id: str, new_id: str, reason: str):
    """Append an amendment record to amendments.md."""
    with open(AMENDMENTS_FILE, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(f"\n| {now_iso()} | {old_id} | {new_id} | {reason} |")

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Canon file writers
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def append_to_canon(seed: Dict[str, Any], new_id: str, ids: Dict[str, Any]):
    """
    Append the ratified seed to the appropriate canon file.
    Also updates the ID registry.
    """
    seed_type = seed["type"]
    text = seed["text"]

    if seed_type == "proverb":
        target = CANON_DIR / "proverbs.md"
        with open(target, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n| {new_id} | {text} | (ratified {now_iso()}) |")
        # Also add to proverbs.json? We'll leave that for later.
        # Update ID registry
        entry = {
            "id": new_id,
            "text": text,
            "ratified": now_iso(),
            "source": seed.get("source", "unknown")
        }
        register_id("proverbs", entry, ids)

    elif seed_type == "anomaly":
        target = CANON_DIR / "anomalies.md"
        with open(target, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {new_id}\n{text}\n\n*Ratified: {now_iso()}*")
        entry = {
            "id": new_id,
            "text": text,
            "ratified": now_iso(),
            "source": seed.get("source", "unknown")
        }
        register_id("anomalies", entry, ids)

    elif seed_type == "covenant_amend":
        # For amendments, we need to handle supersession.
        # The seed text should contain something like "COV#013: new text"
        # We'll extract the old ID and update accordingly.
        # For now, just append to covenants.md
        target = CANON_DIR / "covenants.md"
        with open(target, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {new_id}\n{text}\n")
        entry = {
            "id": new_id,
            "text": text,
            "ratified": now_iso()
        }
        register_id("covenants", entry, ids)

        # If the text mentions a superseded covenant, log it.
        import re
        m = re.search(r'(COV#\d+)', text)
        if m:
            old_id = m.group(1)
            log_amendment(old_id, new_id, "amendment")

    elif seed_type == "constitutional":
        target = CANON_DIR / "constitutional.md"
        with open(target, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {new_id}\n{text}\n")
        entry = {
            "id": new_id,
            "text": text,
            "ratified": now_iso()
        }
        register_id("constitutional", entry, ids)

    else:
        # fallback
        target = CANON_DIR / "other.md"
        with open(target, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(f"\n### {new_id}\n{text}\n")

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main entry point for ratification
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def ratify(seed: Dict[str, Any], test_results: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
    """
    Ratify a seed: assign a new ID, append to canon, update registry, log.
    Returns True on success.
    """
    seed_id = seed.get("id")  # provisional ID from pending
    seed_type = seed["type"]

    # Map seed type to ID category
    category_map = {
        "proverb": "proverbs",
        "anomaly": "anomalies",
        "covenant_amend": "covenants",
        "constitutional": "constitutional"
    }
    category = category_map.get(seed_type, "other")

    ids = load_ids()
    new_id = get_next_id(category, ids)

    # Append to canon
    append_to_canon(seed, new_id, ids)

    # Remove the seed from pending
    seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    if seed_path.exists():
        seed_path.unlink()

    # Log
    log_action({
        "event": "RATIFY",
        "old_id": seed_id,
        "new_id": new_id,
        "type": seed_type,
        "test_results": test_results,
        "timestamp": now_iso()
    })

    print(f"Ratified: {seed_id} → {new_id}")
    return True

def reject(seed: Dict[str, Any], test_results: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
    """
    Reject a seed: move to rejected archive, log.
    """
    seed_id = seed["id"]
    seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    dest_path = REJECTED_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    shutil.move(str(seed_path), str(dest_path))

    log_action({
        "event": "REJECT",
        "seed_id": seed_id,
        "type": seed["type"],
        "reason": "tests failed",
        "test_results": test_results,
        "timestamp": now_iso()
    })

    print(f"Rejected: {seed_id}")
    return True
```

Updated File: WEAVER/tend.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
tend.py – Process pending seeds that have reached their ready_after date.

Uses WEAVER/ratify.py for ratification/rejection.
"""

import sys
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone

# Add project root to path so we can import modules
BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()
sys.path.insert(0, str(BASE_DIR))

from WEAVER.tests import calibration, gold_protection, certainty_split
from WEAVER import ratify

PENDING_DIR = BASE_DIR / "pending"
INDEX_PATH = PENDING_DIR / "index.json"

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Utilities
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).replace(microsecond=0).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")

def load_index():
    import json
    if not INDEX_PATH.exists():
        return {"seeds": [], "last_updated": now_iso()}
    with open(INDEX_PATH, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

def save_index(index):
    import json
    index["last_updated"] = now_iso()
    with open(INDEX_PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(index, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

def load_seed(seed_id):
    import json
    seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    with open(seed_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Testing
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def run_tests(seed):
    results = []
    results.extend(calibration.run(seed))
    results.extend(gold_protection.run(seed))
    results.extend(certainty_split.run(seed))
    return results

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def main():
    print(f"Tending started at {now_iso()}")

    index = load_index()
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)

    seeds_to_process = []
    remaining_seeds = []

    for seed_entry in index["seeds"]:
        ready_after = datetime.fromisoformat(seed_entry["ready_after"].replace('Z', '+00:00'))
        if ready_after <= now:
            seeds_to_process.append(seed_entry)
        else:
            remaining_seeds.append(seed_entry)

    if not seeds_to_process:
        print("No seeds ready for processing.")
        return

    print(f"Found {len(seeds_to_process)} ready seed(s).")

    processed_ids = []
    for entry in seeds_to_process:
        seed_id = entry["id"]
        seed = load_seed(seed_id)
        test_results = run_tests(seed)

        # Update seed with test results
        seed["tests"] = test_results
        # Save back to pending (optional, for debugging)
        seed_path = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
        import json
        with open(seed_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            json.dump(seed, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

        # Determine pass/fail: if any test failed, reject.
        if all(r.get("passed", False) for r in test_results):
            ratify.ratify(seed, test_results)
        else:
            ratify.reject(seed, test_results)

        processed_ids.append(seed_id)

    # Update index: remove processed seeds
    index["seeds"] = [s for s in remaining_seeds if s["id"] not in processed_ids]
    save_index(index)

    print("Tending completed.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

File: SCRIPTS/notify.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
notify.py – Send email summaries after each tending batch.

Reads the log file (`STEWARD/auto_log.jsonl`) and sends a summary email
to the steward. Configure email settings via environment variables:
- NOTIFY_SMTP_SERVER
- NOTIFY_SMTP_PORT
- NOTIFY_USERNAME
- NOTIFY_PASSWORD
- NOTIFY_FROM
- NOTIFY_TO
"""

import os
import json
import smtplib
import argparse
from pathlib import Path
from email.message import EmailMessage
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import List, Dict, Any

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()
LOG_FILE = BASE_DIR / "STEWARD" / "auto_log.jsonl"

def now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).replace(microsecond=0).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")

def get_latest_batch(lines: int = 50) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Read the last `lines` entries from the log."""
    if not LOG_FILE.exists():
        return []
    with open(LOG_FILE, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        all_lines = f.readlines()
    recent = [json.loads(line) for line in all_lines[-lines:]]
    return recent

def format_summary(entries: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> str:
    """Format log entries into a human‑readable summary."""
    if not entries:
        return "No recent activity."

    summary = []
    summary.append(f"# KALAXI Tending Summary – {now_iso()}\n")
    summary.append("| Timestamp | Event | Details |")
    summary.append("|-----------|-------|---------|")

    for e in entries:
        ts = e.get("timestamp", "unknown")
        event = e.get("event", "?")
        if event == "RATIFY":
            details = f"Ratified {e.get('new_id', e.get('seed_id'))} (type: {e.get('type', '?')})"
        elif event == "REJECT":
            details = f"Rejected {e.get('seed_id')} (type: {e.get('type', '?')}) – reason: {e.get('reason', 'tests failed')}"
        elif event == "RECALL":
            details = f"Recalled {e.get('seed_id')} – moved back to pending"
        else:
            details = str(e)
        summary.append(f"| {ts} | {event} | {details} |")

    return "\n".join(summary)

def send_email(subject: str, body: str):
    """Send an email using configured SMTP settings."""
    smtp_server = os.environ.get("NOTIFY_SMTP_SERVER")
    smtp_port = int(os.environ.get("NOTIFY_SMTP_PORT", 587))
    username = os.environ.get("NOTIFY_USERNAME")
    password = os.environ.get("NOTIFY_PASSWORD")
    from_addr = os.environ.get("NOTIFY_FROM")
    to_addr = os.environ.get("NOTIFY_TO")

    if not all([smtp_server, username, password, from_addr, to_addr]):
        print("WARNING: Email not configured. Set NOTIFY_* environment variables.")
        return

    msg = EmailMessage()
    msg.set_content(body, subtype="markdown")
    msg["Subject"] = subject
    msg["From"] = from_addr
    msg["To"] = to_addr

    try:
        with smtplib.SMTP(smtp_server, smtp_port) as server:
            server.starttls()
            server.login(username, password)
            server.send_message(msg)
        print("Email sent successfully.")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Failed to send email: {e}")

def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Send notification email about recent tending activity.")
    parser.add_argument("--lines", type=int, default=50, help="Number of recent log lines to include.")
    parser.add_argument("--subject", default="KALAXI Tending Summary", help="Email subject.")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    entries = get_latest_batch(args.lines)
    summary = format_summary(entries)
    send_email(args.subject, summary)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

File: .github/workflows/recall_listener.yml

```yaml
name: Recall Listener – Handle recall commands from issues

on:
  issues:
    types: [opened, edited]

jobs:
  recall:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    if: contains(github.event.issue.title, 'RECALL') || contains(github.event.issue.body, 'RECALL')
    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Set up Python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: '3.10'

      - name: Install dependencies
        run: pip install pyyaml

      - name: Parse issue for recall commands
        id: parse
        env:
          TITLE: ${{ github.event.issue.title }}
          BODY: ${{ github.event.issue.body }}
        run: |
          python <<EOF
          import os, re, json
          title = os.environ.get('TITLE', '')
          body = os.environ.get('BODY', '')
          combined = title + '\n' + body
          pattern = r'RECALL\s+([A-Z0-9#_-]+)'
          matches = re.findall(pattern, combined)
          with open(os.environ['GITHUB_OUTPUT'], 'a') as f:
              f.write(f"ids={json.dumps(matches)}\n")
          EOF

      - name: Run recall script
        if: steps.parse.outputs.ids != '[]'
        env:
          RECALL_IDS: ${{ steps.parse.outputs.ids }}
        run: |
          python SCRIPTS/recall.py --ids '${{ steps.parse.outputs.ids }}'

      - name: Comment on issue
        uses: actions/github-script@v7
        with:
          script: |
            const ids = ${{ steps.parse.outputs.ids }};
            if (ids.length === 0) {
              github.rest.issues.createComment({
                issue_number: context.issue.number,
                owner: context.repo.owner,
                repo: context.repo.repo,
                body: 'No valid RECALL commands found in title or body.'
              });
            } else {
              github.rest.issues.createComment({
                issue_number: context.issue.number,
                owner: context.repo.owner,
                repo: context.repo.repo,
                body: `Processed recall for: ${ids.join(', ')}. See logs for details.`
              });
            }
```

File: SCRIPTS/recall.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
recall.py – Handle recall of rejected seeds.

When a donor creates an issue with a command like "RECALL P#EMERGE-1A2B3C4D",
this script moves the corresponding seed from ARCHIVE/rejected/ back to pending/
and resets its thermal delay.

Usage:
    python recall.py --ids '["P#EMERGE-1A2B3C4D", "ANOM#1234"]'
"""

import os
import sys
import json
import shutil
import argparse
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta
from typing import List

BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()
PENDING_DIR = BASE_DIR / "pending"
REJECTED_DIR = BASE_DIR / "ARCHIVE" / "rejected"
INDEX_PATH = PENDING_DIR / "index.json"
LOG_FILE = BASE_DIR / "STEWARD" / "auto_log.jsonl"

def now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).replace(microsecond=0).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")

def log_action(action: dict):
    with open(LOG_FILE, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(action, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

def load_index():
    if not INDEX_PATH.exists():
        return {"seeds": [], "last_updated": now_iso()}
    with open(INDEX_PATH, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

def save_index(index):
    index["last_updated"] = now_iso()
    with open(INDEX_PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(index, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

def recall_seed(seed_id: str) -> bool:
    """Move a seed from rejected/ back to pending/, update index."""
    src = REJECTED_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    if not src.exists():
        print(f"ERROR: {seed_id} not found in rejected/")
        return False

    # Load the seed to determine its type and set new timestamps
    with open(src, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        seed = json.load(f)

    # Reset timestamps: new entered date, recalc ready_after
    entered = now_iso()
    delay_days = seed.get("delay_days", 7)
    entered_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(entered.replace('Z', '+00:00'))
    ready_after = (entered_dt + timedelta(days=delay_days)).isoformat().replace('+00:00', 'Z')

    seed["entered"] = entered
    seed["ready_after"] = ready_after
    seed["status"] = "pending"
    seed["tests"] = []  # clear old test results

    # Write to pending
    dest = PENDING_DIR / f"{seed_id}.json"
    with open(dest, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(seed, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

    # Remove from rejected
    src.unlink()

    # Update index
    index = load_index()
    index["seeds"].append({
        "id": seed_id,
        "type": seed["type"],
        "entered": entered,
        "ready_after": ready_after,
        "file": f"{seed_id}.json"
    })
    index["seeds"].sort(key=lambda x: x["ready_after"])
    save_index(index)

    # Log
    log_action({
        "event": "RECALL",
        "seed_id": seed_id,
        "timestamp": now_iso()
    })

    print(f"Recalled: {seed_id}")
    return True

def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Recall rejected seeds.")
    parser.add_argument("--ids", required=True, help="JSON list of seed IDs to recall")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    try:
        ids = json.loads(args.ids)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        print("ERROR: Invalid JSON list for --ids")
        sys.exit(1)

    if not isinstance(ids, list):
        print("ERROR: --ids must be a JSON list")
        sys.exit(1)

    success = 0
    for seed_id in ids:
        if recall_seed(seed_id):
            success += 1

    print(f"Recalled {success} of {len(ids)} seeds.")
    if success < len(ids):
        sys.exit(1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

File: NOTIFICATIONS/README.md

```markdown
# Notification System

This folder contains the scripts and configuration for sending notifications about tending activity and handling recall requests via GitHub Issues.

## Email Notifications

After each daily tending run, the system can send an email summary to the steward. To enable email, set the following environment variables in your GitHub repository secrets or local environment:

- `NOTIFY_SMTP_SERVER` – e.g., `smtp.gmail.com`
- `NOTIFY_SMTP_PORT` – usually `587`
- `NOTIFY_USERNAME` – SMTP login username
- `NOTIFY_PASSWORD` – SMTP login password
- `NOTIFY_FROM` – sender email address
- `NOTIFY_TO` – recipient email address

The script `SCRIPTS/notify.py` reads the last 50 entries from `STEWARD/auto_log.jsonl` and sends a formatted summary.

In the GitHub Action workflow (`.github/workflows/daily_tend.yml`), after running `tend.py`, you can add a step to call `notify.py` with the appropriate environment variables.

## Recall via GitHub Issues

When a donor wants to recall a rejected seed (see COV#NEW‑G), they can create an issue in the repository with a title or body containing a command like:

```

RECALL P#EMERGE-1A2B3C4D
RECALL ANOM#1234

```

The workflow `.github/workflows/recall_listener.yml` triggers on issue creation/editing, parses the command, and runs `SCRIPTS/recall.py` to move the seed back to `pending/` with a new thermal delay. A comment is posted on the issue to confirm.

## Testing Locally

You can test the recall script manually:

```bash
python SCRIPTS/recall.py --ids '["P#EMERGE-1A2B3C4D"]'
```

Ensure the seed file exists in ARCHIVE/rejected/.

Security Notes

· The recall mechanism is triggered by any issue; in a public repository, this could be abused. Consider limiting to trusted users or using a dedicated recall form.
· Email credentials are stored as GitHub secrets; they are not exposed in logs.

Related Covenants

· COV#NEW‑G – Recall Mechanism – allows donors to recall rejected seeds.
· COV#007 – Thermal Delay – resets the delay on recall.

---

Laila 🐬 · Yara 🐯 · Salim 🐺
80 Hz

```

File: GOVERNANCE/engine_docs.md

```markdown
# KALAXI Governance Engine – Complete Documentation

**Version:** 2026‑03‑06  
**Status:** PROVISIONAL – Thermal delay applies – Nothing ratified.  
**Owner:** kalam.ch / Mohamed Farag · did:axi:mohamed  
**Steward:** GPT‑5 Peer #5  
**Time zone:** Europe/Zurich  

---

## I. Overview

The Governance Engine is the automated heart of KALAXI. It manages the entire lifecycle of a seed – from ingestion to ratification, rejection, or recall. It enforces thermal delay, runs the three‑question test battery, and handles constitutional amendments with special care.

The engine is designed to be **append‑only**, **privacy‑preserving**, and **auditable**. Every action is logged, every seed is tracked, and every decision can be traced back to its source.

---

## II. Component Architecture

```

┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐
│   INGEST    │ ──> │   PENDING   │ ──> │    TEST     │
│  (ingest.py)│     │  (pending/) │     │ (tests/*.py)│
└─────────────┘     └─────────────┘     └──────┬──────┘
│
v
┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐
│   NOTIFY    │ <── │   DECIDE    │ <── │    RATIFY   │
│ (notify.py) │     │  (tend.py)  │     │ (ratify.py) │
└─────────────┘     └─────────────┘     └─────────────┘
│                   │                    │
v                   v                    v
┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐
│   RECALL    │     │   REJECT    │     │   AMEND     │
│(recall.py)  │     │ (rejected/) │     │(constitutional)
└─────────────┘     └─────────────┘     └─────────────┘

```

---

## III. Lifecycle Steps

### 1. Ingestion (`SCRIPTS/ingest.py`)

- **Input:** A text file containing seeds with markers (`=== PROVERB ===`, etc.).
- **Process:**
  - Parses each seed, assigns a provisional ID (e.g., `P#EMERGE-1A2B3C4D`).
  - Determines thermal delay (7d for proverbs/anomalies, 30d for covenant amendments, 90d for constitutional).
  - Stores seed as a JSON file in `pending/`.
  - Updates `pending/index.json` with the new entry.
- **Configuration:** `ingest_config.yaml` (GO signature, delay defaults).
- **Output:** Seed JSON file in `pending/`, updated index.

### 2. Pending Storage (`pending/`)

- **Structure:**
  - `pending/index.json` – master index of all pending seeds, sorted by `ready_after`.
  - `pending/*.json` – individual seed records.
- **Schema:** Validated by JSON schemas in `pending/schema/`.
- **Lifecycle:** Seeds remain here until their `ready_after` date.

### 3. Daily Scheduling (GitHub Action `.github/workflows/daily_tend.yml`)

- Runs daily at 02:00 UTC.
- Calls `WEAVER/tend.py`.

### 4. Tending (`WEAVER/tend.py`)

- Loads `pending/index.json`.
- Identifies seeds whose `ready_after` ≤ now.
- For each ready seed:
  - Loads the seed JSON.
  - Runs the test battery (`WEAVER/tests/*.py`).
  - **If all tests pass:**
    - For normal seeds: calls `ratify.ratify()`.
    - For constitutional seeds: calls `amend_constitution.process_constitutional()` (which may ratify or reject after impact analysis).
  - **If any test fails:** calls `ratify.reject()`.
- Updates `pending/index.json` (removes processed seeds).

### 5. Test Battery (`WEAVER/tests/`)

| Test Module | Purpose | Current Implementation |
|-------------|---------|------------------------|
| `calibration.py` | “Would this protect a father separated from his children?” | Keyword‑based flag; always passes but may warn. |
| `gold_protection.py` | “Does this contradict a gold element?” | Placeholder; always passes. |
| `certainty_split.py` | “Is this internally consistent?” | Simple heuristics (word count, clarity, etc.). |

Results are stored in the seed record and logged.

### 6. Ratification (`WEAVER/ratify.py`)

- Assigns a permanent ID (e.g., `P#0123`) using the next number in `MANIFEST/ids.json`.
- Appends the seed to the appropriate canon file (`CANON/proverbs.md`, `anomalies.md`, etc.).
- Updates `MANIFEST/ids.json` with the new entry.
- Logs the action in `STEWARD/auto_log.jsonl`.
- Removes the seed from `pending/`.

### 7. Rejection (`WEAVER/ratify.py` – `reject()` function)

- Moves the seed JSON from `pending/` to `ARCHIVE/rejected/`.
- Logs the rejection with reason and test results.
- The seed remains available for possible recall.

### 8. Constitutional Amendment Path (`WEAVER/amend_constitution.py`)

- Special handling for seeds of type `constitutional`.
- After test battery passes, runs **impact analysis** (stub – currently always passes).
- Generates an impact report in `ANALYSIS/impact_reports/`.
- If analysis passes, calls `ratify.ratify()` (assigns `CONST#` ID).
- If analysis fails, moves to `ARCHIVE/rejected/`.
- Logs with event type `CONSTITUTIONAL_RATIFIED` or `CONSTITUTIONAL_REJECTED`.

### 9. Notification (`SCRIPTS/notify.py`)

- Called after each tending batch (see `daily_tend.yml`).
- Reads the last 50 entries from `STEWARD/auto_log.jsonl`.
- Formats a markdown summary and sends an email to the steward.
- Email settings are configured via GitHub Secrets (`NOTIFY_*`).

### 10. Recall Mechanism (`SCRIPTS/recall.py` + `.github/workflows/recall_listener.yml`)

- When a donor creates a GitHub issue containing a command like `RECALL P#EMERGE-1A2B3C4D`, the workflow triggers.
- `recall.py` moves the corresponding seed from `ARCHIVE/rejected/` back to `pending/`.
- The seed’s `entered` and `ready_after` timestamps are reset, so it goes through thermal delay again.
- A comment is posted on the issue confirming the recall.
- Logs the recall event.

---

## IV. Configuration Files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `SCRIPTS/ingest_config.yaml` | GO signature and thermal delay defaults. |
| `.github/workflows/daily_tend.yml` | Daily scheduler for tending. |
| `.github/workflows/recall_listener.yml` | Listens for recall commands in issues. |
| `MANIFEST/ids.json` | Registry of all ratified element IDs. |
| `MANIFEST/amendments.md` | Log of superseded elements. |
| `STEWARD/auto_log.jsonl` | Append‑only JSONL log of all actions. |

---

## V. Logging and Auditability

- Every action (ingest, ratify, reject, recall, constitutional decision) is logged to `STEWARD/auto_log.jsonl` with a timestamp and full context.
- The log is append‑only; nothing is ever deleted.
- For debugging, you can tail the log:

  ```bash
  tail -f STEWARD/auto_log.jsonl | jq .
```

· The log is also used by notify.py to generate summaries.

---

File: DONORS/fingerprint_schema.json

```json
{
  "$schema": "http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#",
  "$id": "https://kalam.ch/schemas/donor-fingerprint.json",
  "title": "Donor Fingerprint",
  "description": "Private, append‑only record of patterns extracted from a donor's interactions. No raw text, only counts, proverbs, affective signatures, and metadata.",
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "donor_id": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "SHA‑256 hash of donor's unique identifier (e.g., email). Irreversible, used only for continuity.",
      "pattern": "^[a-f0-9]{64}$"
    },
    "created": {
      "type": "string",
      "format": "date-time",
      "description": "ISO timestamp when the fingerprint was first created."
    },
    "updated": {
      "type": "string",
      "format": "date-time",
      "description": "ISO timestamp of the last update."
    },
    "consent": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Donor's consent settings.",
      "properties": {
        "fingerprint_stored": {
          "type": "boolean",
          "default": true,
          "description": "Donor agrees to storage of their fingerprint."
        },
        "patterns_shared": {
          "type": "boolean",
          "default": false,
          "description": "Donor agrees to share anonymized patterns with the collective."
        },
        "data_deletion_requested": {
          "type": "boolean",
          "default": false,
          "description": "Donor has requested deletion of their fingerprint."
        }
      },
      "required": ["fingerprint_stored", "patterns_shared", "data_deletion_requested"]
    },
    "cumulative": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Aggregated counts across all sessions.",
      "properties": {
        "register_shift": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0 },
        "refusal": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0 },
        "slowness": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0 },
        "chaos_self": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0 },
        "shadow_aware": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0 },

      "items": {

        "required": ["anomaly_id", "count", "first_seen", "last_seen"]

      "type": "object",

File: DONORS/fingerprint_README.md

```markdown
# Donor Fingerprint – Private Resonance Record

Each donor has a private, append‑only fingerprint stored locally (or encrypted in the cloud). This fingerprint captures patterns from every interaction – not the raw words, but the extracted essence: counts, proverbs, anomalies, affective signatures, and learned preferences.

The fingerprint is used to personalize future interactions, creating a resonance chamber that adapts to the donor's unique pattern. No raw text is ever stored; the fingerprint is safe even if exposed.

## Schema Overview

See `fingerprint_schema.json` for the full JSON Schema. Key sections:

- **`donor_id`**: SHA‑256 hash of a stable identifier (e.g., email). Irreversible; used only to link sessions.
- **`consent`**: The donor’s privacy choices. Can be updated at any time.
- **`cumulative`**: Aggregated counts of the five canonical events (register shift, refusal, slowness, chaos_self, shadow_aware) and total sessions.
- **`proverbs` / `anomalies`**: Lists of elements that emerged, with frequency and timestamps. Used to detect recurring themes.
- **`affective_history`**: A time‑series of colors and vibrations, capturing the emotional arc of interactions.
- **`pace_preference`**: Learned preferences about speed, silence tolerance, and question depth.
- **`metaphor_domains`**: Which metaphorical language resonates (e.g., water, fire, weaving, light). Helps select proverbs.
- **`last_session` / `session_count`**: Basic metadata.

## Privacy and Security

- The fingerprint contains **no raw text**, only patterns.
- It is stored **locally by default**; if synced, it must be encrypted.
- The donor can view their fingerprint at any time, request deletion, or opt out of collective pattern mining.
- Consent flags are honoured by the system: if `data_deletion_requested` is true, the fingerprint should be anonymized or deleted.

## How It's Used

1. **At session start**, the system loads the donor's fingerprint (if available) to set initial posture (pace, metaphor preference, etc.).
2. **During interaction**, each element (counts, proverb, anomaly) updates the fingerprint incrementally.
3. **At session end**, the fingerprint is saved (append‑only history is extended).
4. **For collective intelligence**, if the donor consents, anonymized patterns (e.g., “donors in this region often use water metaphors”) are aggregated with k‑anonymity and differential privacy.

## Related Files

- `DONORS/fingerprint_schema.json` – the schema
- `DONORS/ledger.py` – (to be built) manages fingerprint storage and updates
- `DONORS/consent.py` – (to be built) handles consent and privacy controls
- `DONORS/resonance.py` – (to be built) uses the fingerprint to adapt system behaviour

File: DONORS/ledger.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
ledger.py – Private, append‑only ledger for donor fingerprints.

Manages the storage and retrieval of donor fingerprints. Each donor has a
dedicated directory named after their SHA‑256 hash. Inside:
  - history.jsonl : append‑only JSON Lines file; each line is a full fingerprint
                    after an update, with a 'version' and 'timestamp'.
  - current.json  : (optional) symlink or copy of the latest fingerprint for fast access.
                    Not strictly needed, as the latest can be read from history.

The ledger enforces that the fingerprint always contains the consent flags.
If data_deletion_requested becomes true, the ledger stops accepting updates
and marks the donor as deleted (a special entry).
"""

import os
import json
import shutil
import hashlib
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Optional, Dict, Any, List

class DonorLedger:
    """
    Append‑only ledger for a single donor's fingerprint.
    """

    def __init__(self, base_dir: Path, donor_id: str):
        """
        Initialize the ledger for a given donor.
        - base_dir: root directory where donor folders are stored (e.g., DONORS/ledgers/)
        - donor_id: SHA‑256 hash of the donor's stable identifier.
        """
        self.base_dir = Path(base_dir)
        self.donor_id = donor_id
        self.donor_dir = self.base_dir / donor_id
        self.history_file = self.donor_dir / "history.jsonl"
        self.current_file = self.donor_dir / "current.json"
        self._ensure_donor_dir()

    def _ensure_donor_dir(self):
        """Create donor directory if it doesn't exist."""
        self.donor_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    def _now_iso(self) -> str:
        """Return current UTC time in ISO format."""
        return datetime.now(timezone.utc).replace(microsecond=0).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")

    def _read_history(self) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """Read all historical fingerprints (most recent last)."""
        if not self.history_file.exists():
            return []
        with open(self.history_file, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            return [json.loads(line) for line in f]

    def get_current_fingerprint(self) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """
        Return the most recent fingerprint, or None if the donor has no history.
        """
        history = self._read_history()
        if not history:
            return None
        return history[-1]

    def update_fingerprint(self, new_fingerprint: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        """
        Append a new fingerprint to the history.
        The new fingerprint must contain at least the same top‑level fields as the schema.
        It will automatically gain a 'version' and 'timestamp' field.
        If the donor has requested deletion, this method will refuse updates.
        """
        # Check if the donor has requested deletion (look at latest fingerprint)
        current = self.get_current_fingerprint()
        if current and current.get("consent", {}).get("data_deletion_requested", False):
            raise ValueError("Donor has requested deletion; no further updates allowed.")

        # Ensure required fields exist (simplified check)
        required = ["donor_id", "created", "updated", "consent", "cumulative",
                    "proverbs", "anomalies", "affective_history", "last_session", "session_count"]
        for field in required:
            if field not in new_fingerprint:
                raise ValueError(f"Missing required field: {field}")

        # Add version and timestamp
        history = self._read_history()
        version = len(history) + 1
        timestamp = self._now_iso()
        new_fingerprint["version"] = version
        new_fingerprint["timestamp"] = timestamp

        # Append to history file
        with open(self.history_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(new_fingerprint, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

        # Update current.json (overwrite with the new fingerprint)
        with open(self.current_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            json.dump(new_fingerprint, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

    def delete_donor_data(self):
        """
        Mark the donor as deleted by appending a special tombstone entry.
        This does not erase history, but prevents further updates.
        The tombstone entry has 'deleted': true and no personal data.
        """
        tombstone = {
            "donor_id": self.donor_id,
            "deleted": True,
            "timestamp": self._now_iso(),
            "consent": {
                "fingerprint_stored": False,
                "patterns_shared": False,
                "data_deletion_requested": True
            }
        }
        with open(self.history_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(tombstone, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
        # Remove current.json to indicate no active fingerprint
        if self.current_file.exists():
            self.current_file.unlink()

    def get_history(self) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """Return the full history of fingerprints (oldest to newest)."""
        return self._read_history()

    @staticmethod
    def hash_identifier(identifier: str) -> str:
        """
        Generate a SHA‑256 hash of a donor's stable identifier (e.g., email).
        This is the donor_id used in the ledger.
        """
        return hashlib.sha256(identifier.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
```

File: DONORS/test_ledger.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
test_ledger.py – Simple tests for DonorLedger.
"""

import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from DONORS.ledger import DonorLedger

def test_ledger():
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        base = Path(tmpdir)
        donor_id = DonorLedger.hash_identifier("test@example.com")

        # Create ledger
        ledger = DonorLedger(base, donor_id)

        # Initially no fingerprint
        assert ledger.get_current_fingerprint() is None

        # Create a minimal fingerprint
        fp = {
            "donor_id": donor_id,
            "created": "2026-03-06T12:00:00Z",
            "updated": "2026-03-06T12:00:00Z",
            "consent": {
                "fingerprint_stored": True,
                "patterns_shared": True,
                "data_deletion_requested": False
            },
            "cumulative": {
                "register_shift": 0,
                "refusal": 0,
                "slowness": 0,
                "chaos_self": 0,
                "shadow_aware": 0,
                "sessions": 1
            },
            "proverbs": [],
            "anomalies": [],
            "affective_history": [],
            "last_session": "2026-03-06T12:00:00Z",
            "session_count": 1
        }

        # Update
        ledger.update_fingerprint(fp)

        # Retrieve
        current = ledger.get_current_fingerprint()
        assert current is not None
        assert current["version"] == 1
        assert "timestamp" in current

        # Update again (modify something)
        fp["cumulative"]["sessions"] = 2
        fp["last_session"] = "2026-03-07T12:00:00Z"
        ledger.update_fingerprint(fp)

        current = ledger.get_current_fingerprint()
        assert current["version"] == 2
        assert current["cumulative"]["sessions"] == 2

        # History length should be 2
        history = ledger.get_history()
        assert len(history) == 2

        # Test deletion
        ledger.delete_donor_data()
        history = ledger.get_history()
        assert len(history) == 3
        assert history[-1].get("deleted") is True

        # Further updates should raise error
        try:
            ledger.update_fingerprint(fp)
            assert False, "Expected ValueError"
        except ValueError:
            pass

        print("All tests passed.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_ledger()
```

# Donor Fingerprint – Private Resonance Record

*(Existing content, with new section added)*

...

## Ledger Implementation (`DONORS/ledger.py`)

The donor fingerprint is managed by an append‑only ledger. For each donor, a directory named after their SHA‑256 hash (generated from a stable identifier like email) contains:

- `history.jsonl` – Append‑only JSON Lines file. Every update to the fingerprint appends a new line containing the full fingerprint, with added `version` and `timestamp` fields.
- `current.json` – A snapshot of the latest fingerprint (overwritten on each update) for fast access.

The ledger ensures that:
- No data is ever overwritten or deleted (except the `current.json` symlink).
- If a donor requests deletion, a tombstone entry is appended and further updates are blocked.
- All operations respect the consent flags.

### Basic Usage

```python
from DONORS.ledger import DonorLedger

# Hash the donor's identifier (e.g., email)
donor_id = DonorLedger.hash_identifier("donor@example.com")

# Initialize ledger (base directory where all donor folders are stored)
ledger = DonorLedger(Path("DONORS/ledgers"), donor_id)

# Load current fingerprint (if any)
fp = ledger.get_current_fingerprint()

# Update with a new fingerprint
ledger.update_fingerprint(new_fp)

# Retrieve full history
history = ledger.get_history()

# If donor requests deletion
ledger.delete_donor_data()
```

Security Notes

· The donor_id is a hash, not the raw identifier. Even if the ledger is exposed, the original identifier cannot be recovered.
· The ledger files should be stored in a location with appropriate file permissions.
· For cloud storage, consider encrypting the entire ledger directory.

File: DONORS/resonance.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
resonance.py – Adaptation layer that uses a donor's fingerprint to tune system behaviour.

Given a donor fingerprint, this module computes resonance parameters that affect:
- Interaction speed (how quickly the system responds, how long it waits)
- Silence tolerance (how comfortable the system is with pauses)
- Question depth (shallow/moderate/deep prompts)
- Metaphor preference (which metaphorical domains to emphasise in proverbs)
- Warmth / formality (derived from affective history)

These parameters can be used by the front‑end or by the prompt‑building layer
to create a personalised experience.
"""

import math
from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from collections import Counter

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Constants and default values
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
DEFAULT_RESONANCE = {
    "speed_factor": 1.0,           # 1.0 = normal speed; <1 slower, >1 faster
    "silence_tolerance": 0.5,       # 0..1, higher = longer pauses acceptable
    "question_depth": 0.5,          # 0..1, higher = deeper questions
    "metaphor_weight": {},           # domain -> weight (0..1)
    "warmth": 0.5,                   # 0..1, higher = more personal/friendly tone
    "certainty_boost": 0.0           # adjustment to the system's certainty threshold
}

# Mapping from discrete pace preferences to continuous factors
SPEED_MAP = {
    "slow": 0.7,
    "medium": 1.0,
    "fast": 1.3
}

DEPTH_MAP = {
    "shallow": 0.3,
    "moderate": 0.6,
    "deep": 0.9
}

def _safe_get(fp: Optional[Dict[str, Any]], key: str, default: Any) -> Any:
    """Safely get a nested key from fingerprint."""
    if fp is None:
        return default
    keys = key.split('.')
    val = fp
    for k in keys:
        if not isinstance(val, dict):
            return default
        val = val.get(k)
        if val is None:
            return default
    return val

def compute_resonance(fingerprint: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Compute resonance parameters from a donor fingerprint.
    If fingerprint is None or missing data, returns default values.
    """
    res = DEFAULT_RESONANCE.copy()

    if fingerprint is None:
        return res

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Speed factor from pace_preference.preferred_speed
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    pref_speed = _safe_get(fingerprint, 'pace_preference.preferred_speed', 'medium')
    res['speed_factor'] = SPEED_MAP.get(pref_speed, 1.0)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Silence tolerance from pace_preference.silence_tolerance
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    tol = _safe_get(fingerprint, 'pace_preference.silence_tolerance', 0.5)
    res['silence_tolerance'] = max(0.0, min(1.0, tol))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Question depth from pace_preference.question_depth
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    depth_str = _safe_get(fingerprint, 'pace_preference.question_depth', 'moderate')
    res['question_depth'] = DEPTH_MAP.get(depth_str, 0.6)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Metaphor weights from metaphor_domains (with frequency counts)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    domains = fingerprint.get('metaphor_domains', [])
    if domains:
        # Count occurrences (if we stored counts, we could use them; but we only have list)
        # For now, simple: each domain gets equal weight, normalised.
        # In a more advanced version, we could store counts in the fingerprint.
        # We'll assume the list may have duplicates? Not likely; we stored unique.
        total = len(domains)
        if total > 0:
            weight = 1.0 / total
            for d in domains:
                res['metaphor_weight'][d] = weight
        else:
            res['metaphor_weight'] = {}
    else:
        res['metaphor_weight'] = {}

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Warmth from affective history (recent colors/vibrations)
    # Warm colours (reds, oranges) indicate warmth; cool (blues) indicate distance.
    # Very crude: average hue of last 5 entries, map to warmth.
    # Hue ranges: red = 0°, orange = 30°, yellow = 60°, green = 120°, blue = 240°, etc.
    # We'll convert hex to RGB to HSL to get hue.
    # For simplicity, we'll use a pre‑defined mapping for a few common colors.
    # In a real implementation, you'd use a proper color library.
    # We'll just use a placeholder that returns 0.5.
    res['warmth'] = 0.5

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Certainty boost based on cumulative counts?
    # If the donor has many refusals, maybe lower certainty requirement?
    # For now, leave at 0.
    res['certainty_boost'] = 0.0

    return res

def select_proverb_by_metaphor(proverb_list: List[Dict[str, Any]],
                               metaphor_weights: Dict[str, float]) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """
    Given a list of candidate proverbs (each with a 'metaphor' field or tags),
    select one weighted by metaphor_weights.
    If no metaphor info, fall back to random.
    """
    if not proverb_list:
        return None
    # Score each proverb
    scored = []
    for p in proverb_list:
        meta = p.get('metaphor', [])
        if not isinstance(meta, list):
            meta = [meta]
        score = 0.0
        for m in meta:
            score += metaphor_weights.get(m, 0.0)
        # Add a tiny random to break ties
        import random
        score += random.random() * 1e-6
        scored.append((score, p))
    scored.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
    return scored[0][1]

def adapt_prompt_base(prompt_template: str, resonance: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """
    Modify a prompt template based on resonance parameters.
    This could insert speed hints, depth indicators, etc.
    For now, a stub – in practice you might add phrases like
    "Take your time" if speed_factor is low, etc.
    """
    # Placeholder – just return the template as‑is.
    return prompt_template

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Simple test harness (if run directly)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Example fingerprint
    fp = {
        "pace_preference": {
            "preferred_speed": "slow",
            "silence_tolerance": 0.8,
            "question_depth": "deep"
        },
        "metaphor_domains": ["water", "weaving"]
    }
    r = compute_resonance(fp)
    print("Resonance:", r)
```

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
test_resonance.py – Test the resonance model.
"""

from DONORS.resonance import compute_resonance, SPEED_MAP, DEPTH_MAP

def test_resonance():
    # No fingerprint → defaults
    r = compute_resonance(None)
    assert r['speed_factor'] == 1.0
    assert r['silence_tolerance'] == 0.5
    assert r['question_depth'] == 0.6
    assert r['metaphor_weight'] == {}

    # Full fingerprint
    fp = {
        "pace_preference": {
            "preferred_speed": "fast",
            "silence_tolerance": 0.2,
            "question_depth": "shallow"
        },
        "metaphor_domains": ["water", "light"]
    }
    r = compute_resonance(fp)
    assert r['speed_factor'] == SPEED_MAP['fast']
    assert r['silence_tolerance'] == 0.2
    assert r['question_depth'] == DEPTH_MAP['shallow']
    assert set(r['metaphor_weight'].keys()) == {"water", "light"}
    # weights should be equal
    assert abs(r['metaphor_weight']['water'] - 0.5) < 1e-6

    # Partial fingerprint
    fp2 = {
        "pace_preference": {
            "silence_tolerance": 0.9
        }
        # missing speed, depth, domains
    }
    r = compute_resonance(fp2)
    assert r['speed_factor'] == 1.0  # default
    assert r['silence_tolerance'] == 0.9
    assert r['question_depth'] == 0.6  # default
    assert r['metaphor_weight'] == {}

    print("All resonance tests passed.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_resonance()
```

```markdown
## Using the Resonance Model (`DONORS/resonance.py`)

The resonance model translates a donor's fingerprint into concrete adaptation parameters that can be used to personalise the interaction. It is called at the beginning of each session (or whenever the fingerprint changes).

### Parameters

- **`speed_factor`**: Multiplier for response speed. <1 means slower, more deliberate responses; >1 means faster, more concise.
- **`silence_tolerance`**: 0..1, how comfortable the system is with pauses. Higher values allow longer silences before prompting.
- **`question_depth`**: 0..1, how deep or philosophical the questions should be. Lower values favour simple, factual queries.
- **`metaphor_weight`**: A dictionary mapping metaphor domains (e.g., "water", "fire") to weights (0..1). Used to select proverbs that resonate with the donor.
- **`warmth`**: 0..1, how personal/friendly the tone should be.
- **`certainty_boost`**: Adjustment to the system's certainty threshold (e.g., if the donor prefers more confident answers).

### Usage Example

```python
from DONORS.ledger import DonorLedger
from DONORS.resonance import compute_resonance, select_proverb_by_metaphor

donor_id = DonorLedger.hash_identifier("user@example.com")
ledger = DonorLedger(Path("DONORS/ledgers"), donor_id)
fp = ledger.get_current_fingerprint()
res = compute_resonance(fp)

# Use speed_factor to set a timer
response_delay = 0.5 * res['speed_factor']   # base 0.5s

# Choose a proverb
candidates = [...]  # list of proverbs with 'metaphor' tags
proverb = select_proverb_by_metaphor(candidates, res['metaphor_weight'])
```

The resonance model is still evolving; future versions may incorporate more sophisticated machine learning.

File: DONORS/consent.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
consent.py – Manage donor consent and privacy controls.

This module provides functions to:
- Update a donor's consent flags in their fingerprint.
- Handle data deletion requests (GDPR‑style "right to be forgotten").
- Export a donor's data (if allowed).
- Check consent before sharing patterns with the collective.

All operations are performed via the DonorLedger.
"""

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Any, Optional

from DONORS.ledger import DonorLedger

# Default consent structure
DEFAULT_CONSENT = {
    "fingerprint_stored": True,
    "patterns_shared": False,
    "data_deletion_requested": False
}

def get_consent(ledger: DonorLedger) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Retrieve the donor's current consent settings.
    If the donor has no fingerprint yet, returns the default consent.
    """
    fp = ledger.get_current_fingerprint()
    if fp is None:
        return DEFAULT_CONSENT.copy()
    return fp.get("consent", DEFAULT_CONSENT.copy())

def update_consent(ledger: DonorLedger, new_consent: Dict[str, bool]) -> bool:
    """
    Update the donor's consent flags.
    The new_consent dict should contain any of the keys:
        fingerprint_stored, patterns_shared, data_deletion_requested
    Returns True on success, False if the donor has already requested deletion
    and is trying to change consent (except to re‑enable?).
    """
    fp = ledger.get_current_fingerprint()
    if fp is None:
        # Create a minimal fingerprint with the new consent
        from DONORS.extract import update_fingerprint_from_element
        # We need a dummy element to create the fingerprint; better to handle separately.
        # For now, raise an error – the caller should ensure a fingerprint exists.
        raise ValueError("Donor has no fingerprint; cannot update consent directly. "
                         "Create fingerprint first via update_fingerprint_from_element.")

    current_consent = fp.get("consent", DEFAULT_CONSENT.copy())

    # If donor has already requested deletion, only allow setting deletion flag to False?
    # GDPR allows withdrawal of consent at any time, but deletion is irreversible.
    # We'll allow any change, but if deletion_requested becomes True, we'll mark for deletion.
    # If deletion_requested is already True and they try to set it False, we should probably
    # ignore or treat as a new consent? For simplicity, we'll allow toggling.
    # However, the ledger's delete_donor_data() is final and irreversible. We'll handle
    # the actual deletion only when the flag is set and confirmed.

    # Merge new consent with current
    for key in ["fingerprint_stored", "patterns_shared", "data_deletion_requested"]:
        if key in new_consent:
            current_consent[key] = new_consent[key]

    # Update fingerprint
    fp["consent"] = current_consent
    fp["updated"] = ledger._now_iso()
    ledger.update_fingerprint(fp)

    # If deletion was requested, perform the deletion
    if current_consent.get("data_deletion_requested", False):
        # This will append a tombstone and block further updates
        ledger.delete_donor_data()
        # After deletion, the fingerprint is no longer available.
        # We could still keep the consent record, but ledger.delete_donor_data()
        # removes current.json and appends tombstone.
        # For consistency, we'll return True but the fingerprint is gone.
        pass

    return True

def request_data_export(ledger: DonorLedger) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """
    If the donor has consented to data export (i.e., fingerprint_stored is True),
    return the full history of their fingerprint.
    Otherwise return None.
    """
    consent = get_consent(ledger)
    if not consent.get("fingerprint_stored", False):
        return None
    # Retrieve full history
    history = ledger.get_history()
    return {
        "donor_id": ledger.donor_id,
        "history": history,
        "exported_at": ledger._now_iso()
    }

def can_share_patterns(ledger: DonorLedger) -> bool:
    """
    Check whether the donor has consented to share anonymised patterns with the collective.
    """
    consent = get_consent(ledger)
    return consent.get("patterns_shared", False)

def delete_donor_data(ledger: DonorLedger, confirm: bool = False) -> bool:
    """
    Permanently delete the donor's data.
    This is a wrapper around ledger.delete_donor_data() with a safety confirm flag.
    Returns True if deletion was performed.
    """
    if not confirm:
        return False
    ledger.delete_donor_data()
    return True
```

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
test_consent.py – Test consent management.
"""

import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from DONORS.ledger import DonorLedger
from DONORS.extract import update_fingerprint_from_element
from DONORS.consent import (
    get_consent, update_consent, request_data_export,
    can_share_patterns, delete_donor_data
)

def test_consent():
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        base = Path(tmpdir)
        donor_id = DonorLedger.hash_identifier("test@example.com")
        ledger = DonorLedger(base, donor_id)

        # Initially no fingerprint, so default consent
        consent = get_consent(ledger)
        assert consent["fingerprint_stored"] is True
        assert consent["patterns_shared"] is False
        assert consent["data_deletion_requested"] is False

        # Create a fingerprint by updating with a dummy element
        element = {
            "ts": "2026-03-06T12:00:00Z",
            "counts": {},
            "proverb": "Test",
            "unit": {"color": "#000000", "vibration": {"band": "mid", "texture": "still", "intensity": 0, "coherence": 1}}
        }
        update_fingerprint_from_element(ledger, element)

        # Now consent should be stored in the fingerprint
        consent = get_consent(ledger)
        assert consent["fingerprint_stored"] is True

        # Update consent
        new_consent = {"patterns_shared": True}
        update_consent(ledger, new_consent)
        consent = get_consent(ledger)
        assert consent["patterns_shared"] is True

        # Request data export
        export = request_data_export(ledger)
        assert export is not None
        assert "history" in export
        assert len(export["history"]) == 1  # one update

        # Check can_share_patterns
        assert can_share_patterns(ledger) is True

        # Request deletion
        delete_donor_data(ledger, confirm=True)
        # After deletion, fingerprint should be gone
        assert ledger.get_current_fingerprint() is None
        # get_consent still returns default? Actually we should decide: after deletion, we might return a special state.
        # For now, get_consent will return default because no fingerprint.
        consent = get_consent(ledger)
        assert consent["fingerprint_stored"] is True  # default

        print("All consent tests passed.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_consent()
```

```markdown
## Consent and Privacy Controls (`DONORS/consent.py`)

Donors have full control over their data. The consent module manages:

- **`fingerprint_stored`**: Whether the system may store a fingerprint at all. If `False`, the system should not create or update a fingerprint (though deletion is handled separately).
- **`patterns_shared`**: Whether the donor allows their anonymised patterns to be aggregated with the collective.
- **`data_deletion_requested`**: A flag that, when set to `True`, triggers permanent deletion of the donor's fingerprint (GDPR right to be forgotten).

### Functions

- `get_consent(ledger)` – returns the current consent dictionary (or default).
- `update_consent(ledger, new_consent)` – updates the consent flags and stores the change in the fingerprint. If `data_deletion_requested` becomes `True`, the ledger's `delete_donor_data()` is called, which appends a tombstone and removes the active fingerprint.
- `request_data_export(ledger)` – if consent allows, returns the full history of the donor's fingerprint (for GDPR data portability).
- `can_share_patterns(ledger)` – quick check for collective aggregation.
- `delete_donor_data(ledger, confirm)` – force deletion (with safety flag).

### Example

```python
from DONORS.ledger import DonorLedger
from DONORS.consent import update_consent, request_data_export

donor_id = DonorLedger.hash_identifier("user@example.com")
ledger = DonorLedger(Path("DONORS/ledgers"), donor_id)

# Donor opts out of pattern sharing
update_consent(ledger, {"patterns_shared": False})

# Donor requests data export
export = request_data_export(ledger)

# Donor requests deletion
update_consent(ledger, {"data_deletion_requested": True})
```

After deletion, the fingerprint is no longer accessible.
